No other job gives you the chance to stay active,   
stay close to the sports you love and be a part of all the 
action! We need more officials in every high school sport. 
 Don’t wait! Sign up to suit up today!
Apply online at HighSchoolOfficials.com

25AL SMITH SPORTSMANSHIP AWARDS  
     The Maryland Public Secondary Schools Athletic Association presents sportsmanship awards annu -
ally at the State Championship meets, a tradition first established in 2016.  The awards are presented  
to the school in each of the four classifications that has demonstrated the highest levels of integrity  
and respect for the sport and their opponents.  Beginning in 2023, the cross country sportsmanship  
awards were named in honor of Al Smith, our beloved announcer at MPSSAA cross country, wrestling,  
and track and field championships, who passed away in March 2023.  Al began announcing as the  
voice of Old Mill High School football and continued to announce for hundreds of events for a time -
frame just shy of 50 consecutive years.  His last event was the cross country state championships in  
the Fall of 2022.  
 
     Coaches, athletes (boys and girls), and spectators from each high school are evaluated and voted on  
by participating coaches.  The Al Smith Sportsmanship Awards are then presented to the winning  
schools during the scheduled awards ceremony for each classification at the State Championship  
Meets.

33PHYLLIS HEMMES SPORTSMANSHIP AWARD  
     The Maryland Public Secondary Schools Athletic Association presents a sportsmanship award annu -
ally at the Field Hockey finals, a tradition established in 2009. The trophy is presented to the one team  
displaying the best overall sportsmanship during the state tournament.  Beginning with the 2022  
championships, the award was named for Phyliss Hemmes, the former long-time director of the state  
field hockey tournament.   
 
     Coaches, players, cheerleaders, and spectators from each participating school are evaluated. Raters  
include state committee members, region directors, officials, and table personnel.  Immediately follow -
ing the last game, the trophy is awarded to the Principal of the school achieving the highest score.  
Previous winners : 
2009  Paint Branch  
2010  River Hill  
2011  Mount Hebron  
2012  Walter Johnson  
2013  Dulaney  
2014  Kent Island  
2015  Kent Island  
2016  South River  
2017  James H. Blake  
2018  Kent Island  
2019  River Hill  
2020  Season cancelled due to COVID-19 pandemic.  
2021 Manchester Valley  
2022 Crofton  
2023 Northern-Cal  
 
 
Congratulations to the 2024 recipients... 
 
              MANCHESTER VALLEY MAVERICKS

3535MPSSAA Football Stats and Records  
Did You Know 
- Only 59 points were scored by the 8 teams in the 1985 Finals, which remains the fewest points scored in the four finals.  
- All four 2016 state titlists were undefeated, marking only the second time (1997) that has happened in MPSSAA history.  
- The most points scored in the Finals, by all teams combined, is 239 — set in 2007 . 
- Quince Orchard’s 40-33 win over North Point in a 2018 Final marked the most points ever scored in a Class 4A title game.  
- Damascus’ playoff appearance in 2017 marked its 20th straight postseason appearance, which is an all-time best in Maryland.  Fast Facts - Records from the Tournament Series

via e-mail at sshealer@msmary.edu .

 MPSSAA Football Championships (Tournament History 1974-2024)
Notes : The MPSSAA football tournament consisted of semifinals and finals  
from 1974 through 1984; quarterfinals, semifinals and finals from 1985  
through 2002; and consisted of region semifinals, region finals, state semifi -
nals and state finals through 2018. Currently, there are two region rounds  
and then state quarterfinals, semifinals, and finals.  
 
Abbreviations : CH-state champion; RU-state runner-up; SF-state semifinalist;  
QF-state quarterfinalist (1985-2002, 2021-present); RR-region runner-up  
(2003-present); RS-region semifinalist (2003-present); RR2-region 2nd  
round; RR1-region 1st round; x-School no longer exists; y-School no longer  
fields a football program; *-placement awarded due to forfeiture . Information: Each school’s total playoff appearances and playoff record  
(state and region) is included in parentheses. Following each year is the clas -
sification in which the school made its postseason appearance, and, from  
2003-present, the region ID (E-East, N-North, S-South, W-West).  
 
Classifications : From 1974 through 1987, Maryland used Class AA, A, B and  
C. Since 1988, the classifications have been titled 4A, 3A, 2A and 1A - work -
ing from largest schools to smallest. Classes 4A/3A and 2A/1A were added  
in 2021 bringing the number of classifications to six.

4545John H. Cox  
“Respect The Game” Football  
Sportsmanship Award 
 
John Cox , former chairman of the MPSSAA Football Committee,  
passed away on October 1, 2007, following a long battle with  
cancer.   
 
John served as football tournament chairman from 1988 to  
2006.  His tenure was marked by two expansions of the state  
football tournament.  The first expansion came in 1985 while he  
was co-chairman of the football committee as the tournament  
moved from four teams per classification to eight teams.  In  
2003, the tournament expanded for a second time, increasing  
to 16 teams per classification.   
 
John also served on the National Federation Football Rules  
Committee as chairman of the Equipment Committee.  His lead -
ership on the rules committee was responsible for many signif -
icant revisions of high school rules.   
 
John’s passion and commitment to the sport of football as well as his sense of fair play and sportsmanship  
leaves a lasting legacy on the MPSSAA and especially the sport he loved.   
 
In his honor, the MPSSAA Football Committee has established the “John H. Cox Respect the Game Football  
Sportsmanship Award.”  The award is presented to the school that displays the best overall sportsmanship  
while competing in the football championship game.  Players, Coaches, Cheerleaders, Bands and Fans can  
Respect the Game by emulating the values John Cox brought to our state.   
                    
                  John H. Cox Football  
                 Sportsmanship Award 
          2024 Recipient: 
      Northern  Garrett Huskies

48Did You Know?   
 
• From 1971 through 1992, classifications were combined. Both a single team champion and a single individual champion were crow ned.  
 
• Winston Churchill was the first team to win back-to-back championships (1987 and 1988).  
 
• The top male one-day score of 67 was held by Brit Musser of Wootton for 25 years when he shot it in the first round of the 19 97 tournament. In 2022,  
    both Noah Wallace of North East-Cecil and Bryan Kim of Sherwood tied the one-day score, with Kim shooting a 67 in both roun ds, but in the same  
    tournament, it was Benjamin Siriboury of River Hill who took the one-day record after shooting a 64 in round one.  Likewise , Siriboury also broke the  
    male two-day record with a score of 132.  He holds the tournament records for both scores.  
 
• Webb Heintzelman of Walt Whitman was the first boy to win 2 individual championships (1977 and 1979). No other boy has won mo re than two.   
 
• In 1984, the first  individual girls’ champion was crowned - Clare Dolan of Seneca Valley. Dolan held the records for the one -day (74) and two-day total  
    (149) for 17 years.  
 
• Senior Helen Yeung of River Hill set new female records at the 2023 tournament. Her one-day score of 65 was shot on Day 1 and  after shooting a   
    67 on Day 2, she ultimately set a new two-day record score of 132.  
 
• Kim Cayce of Winston Churchill (1986, 1987, and 1988), Stephanie Connelly of Northeast (2001, 2002, 2004), and Bailey Davis ( 2017, 2018, 2019)  
    of North Point are the only females to win 3 individual titles.  
 
•Winston Churchill established new records for both a one-day low team score (283) AND a two-day low team score (572) at the 2015 tournament.  
 
•Marriotts Ridge set the record for the most consecutive state titles with seven in 2018.

64STEVE MALONE SPORTSMANSHIP AWARDS  
     The Maryland Public Secondary Schools Athletic Association will present sportsmanship awards annually at the State Soccer Final s, a 
tradition established in 2002. Beginning in 2012, the Soccer Committees renamed these prestigious awards the Steve Malone Sport s-
manship Awards, in honor of Steve Malone, longtime member of the MPSSAA Soccer Committee and Director of the State Soccer Tourn a-
ment.  Steve worked with the State Soccer Committee for thirty-eight years as a District V Representative, as the Director of t he Boys  
Soccer Committee, and as the State Tournament Director.  Additionally, Steve served as the MPSSAA State Soccer Rules Interprete r for 
twenty years along with twelve years on the NFHS Soccer Rules Committee.  
 
     Players, Coaches, Officials, and Fans can Respect the Game by emulating the values that Steve Malone brought to our state.     
Previous winners : 
2002 Boys—Winston Churchill 2002 Girls—Century 2015 Boys—Meade 2015 Girls—Bel Air

 Rally Score Card - Tournament Records 2003-2022
The change of format for the Volleyball tournament in 1990 al -
lowed the MPSSAA an opportunity  to recognize individual and  
team accomplishments. The MPSSAA is most grateful to Lisa  
Meyer of Owings Mills, for many years the official statistician  
for the championships, who generously volunteered to com -
pile and maintain the records.  
 
 
 When the state semifinals moved to a central site in 1996,  
those performances were also included in the statistics  
(sf=semifinal; f=final).  The Side Out Records in this issue re -
flect those set from 1990 through 2002.  
 
Since the adoption of rally scoring in 2003, a new list of Rally  
Scoring tournament records now appear on these pages.

75LESLEY W. COOKE SPORTSMANSHIP AWARD  
The Maryland Public Secondary Schools Athletic Association presents annually a sportsmanship award at the Volleyball fi -
nals, a tradition established in 1998. This prestigious award is now named to honor the memory of Lesley W. Cooke, Athletic  
Director at McDonough High School and long-time member of the State Volleyball Committee who passed away suddenly in  
the fall of 2004. The trophy will be presented to the one school that displays the best overall sportsmanship while participat -
ing in the state tournament.  
 
Coaches, players, cheerleaders, and spectators from each participating school are evaluated and rated by members of the  
state tournament committee during each tournament match. At the conclusion of the final match, a commemorative plaque  
is presented to the Principal of the school achieving the highest overall average score.  
  
Previous winners : 
1998 Centennial  
1999 Northern-Cal  
2000 Fallston  
2001 Gaithersburg  
2002 James M. Bennett
